## 1 General 

## 1.1 Background 

According to the Circular of National Development and Reform Commission on Approval of Establishing Pilot Area for National Urban-rural Integration Reform in Chongqing and Chengdu issued on June 7, 2007, Chongqing was approved as the pilot area for national urban-rural integration reform. On January 26, 2009, the General Office of the State Council issued the Opinions of the State Council on Promoting Chongqing Urban-rural Integration Reform and Development (GF [2009] No. 3), which covers general requirements on promoting urban and rural integration reform and development in Chongqing, making the migrants with a stable life and well-off in order to ensure harmonious development of the Three Gorges reservoir area, developing modern agriculture and boosting construction of new countryside, accelerating the reform of old industrial bases and striving to develop modern service industry, opening wider to the outside world and developing open inland economy, accelerating infrastructure construction and improving the urban and rural development capacity, strengthening resource conservation and environment protection and speeding up efforts to change the development mode, making effort to develop social undertakings and improving public service level, positively carrying forward experiment in reform and establishing a system for urban and rural integration development. The State Council requests Chongqing to accelerate the urban and rural integration development and reform to set an example for the whole nation. This is a significant strategic deployment of the country to boost development of the Middle and West China and also an important measure to implement scientific outlook on development and to promote development of a harmonious society. A mission led by the World Bank (hereinafter called “WB”) Director for Core Business in Asia-Pacific Region and WB Deputy Country Director for China came to Chongqing at the end of June 2007 to investigate the implementation of several WB projects under construction in Chongqing and the preparation for urban and rural integration development. They thought highly of the implementation of the WB projects in Chongqing and once again expressed the willingness of providing loans to aid the urban and rural integration development of Chongqing. The WB president indicated, when he paid a visit to Chongqing on December 25, 2007, that the construction of pilot area for urban and rural integration development in Chongqing was consistent with policies and objectives of WB loan, and WB was therefore willing to cooperate with Chongqing and to create a typical cooperation mode. WB and the Chongqing government Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 2have been in cooperation for nearly 20 years. All pilot projects in Chongqing received high appraisal from WB. The World Bank mission identified the Chongqing Urban-rural Integration Project (hereinafter called “CURIP”) in preparation that were to be granted with WB loans in Chongqing during December 8-12, 2008 and pre-appraised the project during June 15-19, 2009. 

## 1.1.1 Overview of Chongqing Municipality 

Chongqing Municipality covers an area of 82,400 km² and has 40 administrative districts and counties (autonomous counties), including 19 districts of Wanzhou, Fuling, Yuzhong, Dadukou, Jiangbei, Shapingba, Jiulongpo, Nan’an, Beibei, Wansheng, Shuangqiao, Yubei, Banan, Qianjiang, Changshou, Jiangjin, Hechuan, Yongchuan and Nanchuan, and 21 counties (autonomous counties) of Qijiang, Tongnan, Tongliang, Dazu, Rongchang, Bishan, Liangping, Chengkou, Fengdu, Dianjiang, Wulong, Zhongxian, Kaixian, Yunyang, Fengjie, Wushan, Wuxi, Shizu, Xiushan, Youyang and Pengshui (the later 4 are autonomous counties). Chongqing, taking the city proper as the core which is surrounded by the districts and counties (autonomous counties), forms an modern urban network with organic combination of large, medium and small sized cities. It is now the largest metropolis in China with most population and administration units. Chongqing has a registered population of 32,353,200 (by the end of 2007). Most population is of Han Nationality. There are also 54 minority nationalities like Tujia Nationality and Miao Nationality with the population 2,103,000 in total, accounting for 6.5% of the total population, including 1,424,000 of Tujia Nationality and 502,000 of Miao Nationality. Chongqing is a combination of both large urban and rural areas. Its rural areas are wide with large population, huge potential for developing agricultural resources and arable land of 2,235,900 hectares. Chongqing has complex landform, varied geomorphic and abundant resources. It is the major grain production area and the production base of commodity pork. By seizing the historic opportunities of becoming the municipality directly under the central government, construction of the Three Gorges Project and the Western Development, Chongqing kept adjusting greatly the economic structure, opening wide to the outside world, intensifing institutional reform and accelerating infrastructure construction. It witnessed the Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 3overall development of economy and society and increased further the comprehensive strength. It is ranked within the top five among the 12 provinces, autonomous regions and municipality in terms of major economic indicators. Chongqing is a municipality featuring dualistic urban-rural structure. The rural area covers a large area, agriculture takes a large proportion and there are still lots of surplus rural labor there. There is large gap of income distribution and development stage between urban and rural areas and among different areas. Such disparity is continuing increasing: during 2000-2006, the income gap between urban and rural residents increased from 3.32:1 to 3.65:1, and the GDP gap among the developed economic zone of the city, the West-Chongqing Economic Hallway and the ecological economic zone in the Three Gorges Reservoir increased from 1.26:1 and 1.29:1 ( in 2002) to 1.49:1 and 1.43:1 (in 2006). 

## 1.1.2 Government Strategies for Urban and Rural Integration Development 

The Municipal Party Committee and Municipal Government of Chongqing put forward the 

development strategy of “one circle and two wings” at 2007 economic work meeting, aiming at developing a economic circle centered on Chongqing’s city proper with a radius of one-hour commuting distance (one circle), and accelerating the construction of the North East Chongqing centered on Wanzhou with the main body of the area of Three Gorges Reservoir and the poorer South East Chongqing centered on Qianjiang with ethnic minorities concentrating (two wings), in order to form a new pattern in which areas could realize balanced development and have complementary advantages. This strategy will help promote Chongqing to transfer from the pattern of “large urban area leading large rural area” to “urban-rural integration development and reform”, facilitate the establishment of economic growth pole in West China in cooperation with Chengdu-Chongqing Economic Zone and narrow the gap between the rural and urban residents in terms of public goods such as education, culture, social security, health care and infrastructure etc. In 2008, Chongqing Municipal Party Committee brought forth the idea of construction of “Five Chongqings” including Livable Chongqing, Green Chongqing, Smooth Chongqing, Healthy Chongqing and Safe Chongqing, which is a practical and new road for characteristic scientific development of Chongqing. Its objectives are derived from the five major strategic missions contained in the Opinions of the State Council on Promoting Chongqing Urban-rural Integration Reform and Development , including “one circle and two wings” development Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 4strategy, strategy on opening the inland area wider to outside world, strategy on industrial upgrading and optimization, supportive strategy on vitalizing Chongqing with science and education and strategy on resource conservation and environmental protection. 

## 1.2 Overview of CURIP 

CURIP can be divided into the following four categories, comprising 16 sub-components: 

Category 1 : urban and rural infrastructure, including seven road and water supply sub-components, including four road sub-components above Class 3 and three water supply sub-components at town level; 

Category 2 : employment capacity upgrade for migrant workers, including four sub-components of employment transfer training base for rural surplus labor; 

Category 3 : new countryside demonstration, including three sub-components of village lane/road, water supply and comprehensive service center; and 

Category 4 : basic health care system, including two sub-components. Please see Fig. 1-1 for distribution of sub-components in districts and counties. Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 5Fig. 1-1 Distribution of Sub-components in Districts and Counties under CURIP 

## 1.3 Project Financing 

The financial, technical and intelligent supports are needed by CURIP. It is affirmed that WB will provide a loan of USD 84 million to Chongqing for the project. In addition, it also plans to make good cooperation with Chongqing in the aspects of technical assistance and knowledge sharing.

## 1.4 Basis for EIA (Environmental Impact Assessment) Report Preparation 1.4.1 Laws and Regulations of the PRC 

Applicable laws and regulations mainly include: 

a. Environmental Protection Law of the People’s Republic of China; b. Law of the People’s Republic of China on the Prevention and Control of Atmospheric Pollution; c. Law of the People's Republic of China on Prevention and Control of Water Pollution; d. Law of the People's Republic of China on the Prevention and Control of Pollution by Solid Waste; e. Law of the People’s Republic of China on the Prevention and Control of Environmental Noise Pollution; f. Law of the Peoples Republic of China on Soil Conservation; g. Law of the Peoples Republic of China on Urban Planning; h. Highway Law of the People's Republic of China; i. Law of the People's Republic of China on the Promotion of Clean Production; j. Law of the People's Republic of China on Environmental Impact Assessment; k. Regulations on the Protection of Basic Farmland; 

l. Opinions of the State Council on Promoting Chongqing Urban-rural Integration Reform and Development (GF [2009] No.3); m. Decree of the State Council of the PRC [1998] No. 253: Regulations on the Administration of Construction Project Environmental Protection; 

n. Decree of the Ministry of Environmental Protection of the PRC [2008] No. 2: Classification of Construction Project Lists for EIAs; and 

o. Environment Protection Bureau of State Environmental Protection Administration of the PRC [1993] No. 324: Provisions of Strengthening EIA Management of Construction Projects Financed by International Financial Organizations. 

## 1.4.2 WB Requirements 

WB requirements cover ten basic safeguard policies, in which the EIA policy (OP4. 01) is the fundamental requirements and the major bases for EIA report preparation. The proposed project does not involve international waterway specified in OP7.50 and is not located in any disputed area defined in OP7.60, so these two policies will not be applicable for assessment. Other policies will be adopted in the assessment after proper investigation, analysis and selection. Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 7

## 1.4.3 Sector Regulations of the PRC 

a. Technical Standard of Highway Engineering (JTG B01-2003); b. Code for Municipal Road Design (CJJ 37-1990); c. Code for Design on Accessibility of Urban Roads and Buildings (JGJ 50-2001); d. Code for Planting Planning and Design on Urban Road (CJJ 75-97); e. Code for Comprehensive Planning of Urban Engineering Pipeline (GB 50289-98); f. Administration Methods for Environmental Protection in Transportation Construction Project (Decree of the Ministry of Transport of the PRC [2003] No. 5); g. Technical Codes of Medical Waste Centralized Disposal (HF [2003] No. 206); h. National Inventory of Hazardous Waste (Decree of the Ministry of Environmental Protection of the PRC and National Development and Reform Commission [2008] No. 1); i. Measures on the Management of Duplicated Form for Transfer of Hazardous Waste (Decree of the State Environmental Protection Administration of the PRC [1999] No. 5); j. Circular of the General Office of the State Council Concerning the Implementation of Relevant Policies and Measures in the Opinions of the General Office of CCCPC and the State Council on Promoting the Building of a New Socialist Countryside (GBH [2006] No.13); k. Circular of the General Office of the State Council Concerning Strengthening the Work of Ensuring the Safety of Drinking Water (GBF [2005] No.45); l. Standard of Packaging Bags, Containers and Warning Symbols Specific to Medical Waste (HJ/T 421-2008); m. Technical Standard for Medical Waste Incinerator (GB19218-2003); n. Standard for Pollution Control on Hazardous Waste Storage (GB18597-2001); and o. Sanitary Standard for Drinking Water (GB 5749-2006).

## 1.5 EIA Summary 1.5.1 Project EIA Classification 

One of the major objectives of the proposed project, i.e. CURIP, is to improve the infrastructure facilities and raise the public service level for districts, counties, towns and villages of Chongqing, so as to lay the foundation for developing economy of poverty-stricken areas, improving the living standard of town residents and narrowing the urban-rural gap. After site investigation by WB experts in EIA, project team from Chongqing Project Management Office (hereinafter called “CPMO” and sub-component EIA agencies, there is no environmental sensitive area sucha as ecological sensitive and fragile area, area requiring special protection Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 9and area requiring social attention in project area. Meanwhile, the sub-components scatter in the whole municipality and individual investment is small. CPMO consultation experts in EIA and WB experts in EIA from the safeguard commission suggest that the proposed project is classified into Category B project in terms of environment screening which has been affirmed by the WB. 

## 1.5.2 Preparation of EIA Report for Sub-component 

Since CURIP will apply for WB loan, the EIA documents for sub-component shall not only meet the requirements for domestic EIA approval but also comply with the safeguard policies of the WB. In response to suggestions of the WB experts in EIA from the safeguard mission, the CPMO has organized trainings for project implementation units (hereinafter called “PIU”), design institutes and consultants, so as to make them be familiar with policies and requirements of the WB, take the WB safeguard policies into consideration and reflect in their EIA documents. All agencies that carry out or participate in EIA for sub-components under CURIP have been granted the qualification certificates for EIA by the Ministry of Environmental Protection of the PRC and have researchers and engineers in such diverse fields as sociology, human geography, ecology, environmental engineering and traffic engineering. Some EIA agencies have undertaken successively the EIA work for Chongqing Environmental Project (CUEP)and Chongqing Small Cities Infrastructure Improvement Project (CSCP). There are totally 15 EIA documents for sub-components under CURIP (including 2 EIA reports, 8 EIA report sheets and 5 registration forms for environmental impact). They have been approved by or filed with municipal or district or county Environmental Protection Bureau of Chongqing according to requirements on national EIA classification. Please see Table 1-1 for summary of sub-components under CURIP and related EIA documents. ## 1.5.4 Assessment Standards and Environmental Protection Objective 

The assessment standards and environmental protection objective are based on the assessment standards for proposed project area of Chongqing Environmental Protection Bureau and environmental protection bureau of relevant districts and counties and the WB safeguard polices. 

1.5.4.1 Standards on Environmental Quality 

The current national standards shall be applicable for quality assessment of air environment, acoustic environment and surface water environment and assessment of sanitation indicator for drinking water. Please see Annex A for details. 

1.5.4.2 Discharge Standards 

Please see Annex A for discharge and emission standards of waste water, waste gas, noise and other pollutants. 

1.5.4.3 Soil Conservation 

Soil conservation shall be subject to Standards for Classification and Gradation of Soil Erosion 

(SL190-96). Please see Annex A for details. 

1.5.4.4 Environmental Protection Objective and Environmental Sensitive Sites 

a) Environmental Protection Objective According to site survey on areas along and surrounding the proposed sub-components and status investigation on social and ecological environment by sub-component EIA agencies, the general conclusion reached as: each sub-components assessment areas does not involve any nature reserve, wild animal or plants protected by the state, or place of historical interest and heritage conservation unit. According to the nature of each sub-component and characteristics of the surrounding environment, the environmental protection objectives are set as follow: (1) Try the best to mitigate impact of noise, waste gas and dust generated during the construction period on the environmental sensitive sites such as centralized residential Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 15 areas, schools, hospitals and water sources nearby. Take engineering measures and environmental protection measures to mitigate impacts on ecological environment and impact on surface water and ground water such as soil erosion and vegetation deterioration. (2) After the project implementation, the quality of surface water and air environment will be improved. The quality of acoustic environment will not be obviously interior to the current condition. (3) Avoid and mitigate adverse impact on natural and social resource in areas where the project is located; (4) Restore vegetation influenced by construction, enhance soil conservation level and maintain integration of the ecological system. b) Environmental Sensitive Site According to analysis on site investigation, survey and monitoring result, the environmental sensitive sites of CURIP are set in Table 1-3. With the permission and study of the Bureau of Ethnics and Religion of Yongchuan District, Chongqing on September, 2007, the Benzun Temple is allowed to be restored as a Buddhist site.( the Bureau of Ethnics and Religio n of Yongchuan District[2007]No.010) . Therefore, the Road Project Const ruction of the Banqiao Town, Yongchuan District of the CURIP is not violation to the OP4.11 Material Cultural Article of the security policy of the Bank. The disposition of the cultural relics management bureau of Yongchuan on the Benzun Templ e Level of the Banqiao Town and the directions on the restoration of it as a Buddhist site by the Bureau of Ethics and Religion of the Yongchuan District, Chongqing is added to the Annex C.

## 2 Natural Conditions and Baseline of Regional Environment 

## 2.1 Geographic Location 

Located in the southwest of inland of China, on the upper reaches of Yangtze River and at the eastern border of Sichuan Basin, Chongqing sits at the transition areas bordering Qinghai-Tibetan Plateau and the Middle and Lower Yangtze Valley Plain within the range of 105°11'E-110°11'E and 28°10'N-32°13'N. Chongqing, extending 470km in the east to west and 450km in the north to south, borders Hubei and Hunan Provinces in the east, Guizhou Province in the south, Sichuan Province in the west and Shaanxi Province in the north, with total areas of 82,400km 2, including 647.78km 2 of urban areas. Please see Table 2-1 for the districts and counties where the sub-components under CRUIP are and geographic locations. ## 2.2 Natural Conditions 

The subtropical monsoon humid climate is dominant in Chongqing, presenting a moderate climate, where annual temperature in average remains at 18 and lowest temperature in the winter sits at 6-8 . Typically, it is pretty hot in Chongqing in the summer, with daytime max. temperature more than 35 over July and August and total hours of sunshine of 1000-1200hr. It is warm in winter and hot in summer. There is a long frost-free season. Abundant rainfall of as high as 1000-1450mm precipitation across the year always occurs. Situated in the east of Sichuan Basin, Chongqing is surrounded respectively by Daba Mountain, Wushan Mountain, Wuling Mountain and Dalou Mountain in the north, east and south, where hills and mountains are the dominating geomorphic plus large areas of hillside fields. Chongqing is thus renowned as Mountain City. Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 24 The regions where the sub-components are built exhibit simple geological structure and no fault inside. There is no fault, landslide, deformation crack or collapse identified within the range of sub-component sites, thus constituting an acceptable overall stability, where seismic intensity VI degree is defined. The rivers running through Chongqing include the Yangtze River, Jialing River, Wujiang River, Fujiang River, Qijiang River and Daning River etc. The Yangtze River main stream travels through Chongqing from west to east with a total distance of 665km. Please see Table 2-2 for natural conditions of landform, geomorphic, meteorology, river, hydrology in the regions of sub-components under CRUIP. Table 2-2 Regional Natural Conditions of Sub-component under CURIP 

District/ County Name of Sub-component Related Landform, Geomorphic, Meteorology, River and Hydrology 

Xiushan County 

Mie’ao to Liangting Road Project Phase I Xiushan County is dominated by flat grounds, low mountain hills and low-middle hills, which geological structure is Neocathaysian tectonic system and Cathaysian system. Xiushan is located in the southern edge of subsidence formation in Sichuan and Hunan across the Yangtze tableland, exhibiting nondevelopment and under-development of rock crevice, and no faults and fracture zones. The proposed road will run through the hills, with the slope of 10~60°, where mudslide, landslide and solution crevice are not developed. There are stable rocky slope and small thickness of soil layer. Thus, it is a region with acceptable overall stability. Meijiang River is a major river across the region. The subtropical monsoon humid climate is defined in the region, with abundant precipitation and distinct seasons. Banan District Huimin-Yipin Road The region is dominated by parallel ridges and valleys in landform and low mountains in geomorphic. The subtropical SE monsoon humid climate is defined in the region, with abundant precipitation, majority at night and Autumn. The average temperature over the years sits at 18.5   in the region, where dominating wind is N, NNE at max. velocity of 

26.7m/s. Yipin River, the Yangtze River primary tributary, is a main river along Tiaoshi to Yipin line, which originates in the north of Qijiang County, meets the Yangtze River in Banan Yudong Town, traveling for a distance of 48km. Wanshou River, a tributary of Yuxi River - the Yangtze River primary tributary, is a main river along Huimin to Nanpeng line. In the Huimin to Yipin Road Laodingyan Tunnel region, the water-bearing layer in the underground water includes soil pore water water-bearing layer and sandrock pore water water-bearing layer. The soils in the tunnel site cover clay and mild clay distributed in scattered pattern, in thickness of 0-1m. The underground water is buried in a depth far larger than the thickness of soil layer. Thus, the soil pore water water-bearing layer may contain small amount of perched water at the time of rain, i.e. seasonal water-bearing layer. The tunnel wall rock is mainly sandrocks and mudrocks of the Upper Shaximiao Formation of Middle Jurassic System. The shallow weather crevices are seasonal water-bearing layer, with filtration coefficient of K=0.206m/d. There is low capacity of water-bearing and diversion in the layer so that atmospheric precipitation is a main source for underground feed. The sloped contour in the tunnel site is good for drain of atmospheric precipitation, majority of which is drained to the low spots in gravity along the slope and only minority will filtrate into the water-bearing layer via surface weathered crevices to form underground water. Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 25 

District/ County Name of Sub-component Related Landform, Geomorphic, Meteorology, River and Hydrology 

Tongliang County Urban municipal road project The project is situated in the areas linking the hills in the west of Chongqing and parallel ridge and valley in the east of Chongqing, where low mountains, hills, shallow hills are the most common relief. There are no faults, landslides, secondary folds, collapses identified within the region, with seismic intensity of VI degree. The subtropical monsoon humid climate is defined in the region with abundant precipitation and distinct seasons. The annual average temperature is 17 ~18.8 , precipitation is 969.5~1293.8mm and wind velocity is 1.1-1.7m/s. The developed surface runoff in the project region is identified. The waters in the region cover one stream, two brooks and three rivers traveling through the region, i.e. Fujiang River, Major An Brook (Qiongjiang River), Minor An Brook, Huaiyuan River, Jiuyuan River and Pingtan River. The Fujiang River within Tongliang County runs for a distance of 23km, the biggest water source for the county. Major An Brook (Qiongjiang River) travels for a distance of 37km within the county, the 2 nd largest water source. Minor An Brook travels for a distance of 88.3km within the county, the 3 rd largest water source and the most valuable one for utilization in the county. Since the relief across the county exhibits high spots in the southeast and low ones in the northwest, surface water is well drained. Underground water, mainly bicarbonate, calcium sulfate, magnesium sulfate in water quality, is less contaminated and receives precipitation as recharge, which is drained to the ground via overfilling and spring. Yongchuan District Construction of road infrastructures in Banqiao Town Banqiao Town is located in the low-mountain hills with complex landforms and geomorphic. The geomorphic along the road may be split into two categories based on the genetic classification, i.e. abrasion geomorphic and accumulation one. The abrasion geomorphic is characterized by smooth relief, much debris at the bottom of valleys and small relative height difference. The accumulation geomorphic is characterized by flattened ground and thickness of debris likely to be affected by the foundation structure and landform. The subtropical monsoon humid climate is defined in the region with abundant precipitation and distinct seasons. The annual average temperature is 17.8 and precipitation is 1036mm. Abundance of water resources is identified in the region, with Minor An Brook across the Town for a distance of 15km. Liuxi River terminates within the Town and meets the Minor An Brook. Fengjie County Zhuyi water supply project Sichuan Basin eastern landform configuration is dominated in Fengjie, where the Yangtze River runs through the territory in the middle, and rolling mountains and widely-distributed valleys are available. The mountains in the region account for 88.3% of total and moderate mountains (higher than 1000m at sea level) 80.01% of total. The overall landforms in the county see high spots in the southeast and northeast, and smooth ones in the middle and west. The south and north banks are symmetrically distributed across the Yangtze River. The higher spots at sea level, the farther from the Yangtze River. There are small amount of smooth river valleys and flat grounds. Zhuyi Town, 6km from Fengjie city in the east, is located at Zhuyi areas to the east of Zhuyi River at the north bank of Yangtze River. The subtropical monsoon humid climate is defined in the region with moderate climate, abundant precipitation and distinct seasons. The annual average precipitation is 1132.2mm and temperature is 16.4 at areas with sea level of lower than 600m and less than 10.8 at areas with sea level of higher than 1400m. The Yangtze River main stream runs for 41.5km in the middle of the territory. All rivers and streams within the territory belong to the Yangtze River system, including Meixi River, Daxi River, Shishun River, Zhuyi river, etc. There are 17 main streams and tributaries running through the region, including the Yangtze River and other ones with basin area of more than 50km 2 . Such streams and rivers have cutting density of 0.43km 2 at average. There are abundant water resources, with hydroenergy reserves amounting to 340,000kW and workable reserves of 97,000kW. Zhuyi areas surface water Zhuyi River belongs to the Yangtze River system, which average flow rate totals 2.92m 3 /s annually. The underground water is mainly consisting of the Quaternary overburden pore water and bedrock weathered crevice water. Complete marlite, shale, sandstone constitute a relative water-resisting layer. The underground water is fed from filtration of atmospheric Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 26 

District/ County Name of Sub-component Related Landform, Geomorphic, Meteorology, River and Hydrology 

precipitation and surface water. High relief in the northwest and low one in the southeast within the project site benefit runoff drain of surface water and underground water. Shizhu County Huangshui water supply project Huangshui Town is dominated by moderate-mountain highlands, as well as deep valleys and grounds. Surrounded by neighboring mountains and rolling hills, the Huangshui Town has average sea level of 1550.14m. There are no landslides, unsafe rocks, mudslides, settlement, collapses identified in the project region. Surveys show undevelopment of defective geological condition. The subtropical monsoon humid climate is defined in the region with moderate climate, abundant precipitation and distinct seasons. The annual average temperature is 10.4 with average 20.1 max. in July and 0.4 min. in Jan. and annual average precipitation is 1372.6mm. The Longhe River, covering a basin area of 1506.95km 2 and located in the upper reaches of Huangshui Town, flows for 104.7km within the Town at average flow rate of 33.9m 3 /s and average runoff flow rate of 1.007billion m 3 for many years. The underground water in Huangshui Town, Shizhu County, is dominated by loose rock pore water and fragmental rock pore crevice water. The loose rock pore water is mainly distributed in the Jialing River terrace pebbly sand and loose debris, characterized by low capacity of enriched water, perfect filtration and seasonal immersion. The fragmental rock pore crevice water is distributed in the Jurassic system sand shale layer. The Jurassic formation is dominated by mudrocks. The sandrock has small thickness and poor water-storing capacity, which acts as a relative water-resisting layer. Thus, there is low amount of such underground water. Xiushan County Shiye, Rongxi and Longchi water supply project Xiushan County is situated in a region with big difference in heights, i.e. high in the southwest and low in the northeast, and rolling mountain valleys and widely distributed streams as well. There are widely scattered hill depression and shallow plains. Shiye and Longchi water supply project is to be built in a region shaped in deep hills and provided with a slope extending from the high north to the low south. There are no faults, landslides, mudslides, etc., exhibiting good stability in geology. The region has seismic intensity of VI degree. The subtropical monsoon humid climate is defined in the region with moderate climate, abundant precipitation and distinct seasons. The annual average temperature is 16.5 and precipitation is 1200-1300mm. The northward wind prevails. Main rivers in Xiushan County include Meijiang River, Pingjiang River, Rongxi River, which all belong to the Meijiang River system. The underground water is mainly carbonate karst water, followed by fragmental rock crevice water and the Quaternary loose pore water, which is fed from precipitation and drained to river valleys and depressions in the way of spring water. Qijiang County New Countryside Demonstration in Qingshui village in Gunan Town, Xishan village in Sanjiang town and Chuijiao village in Datong town Low-mountains and hills are the most common landforms in Qijiang County, where there is high relief in the south and west and low in the east and north. The heavy-hilly areas are seen in the project region, classified as structural denudation hilly landform, and characterized by big relief change with max. slope of 50  and relative height difference of 

10-40m. The project region has simple engineering geological conditions, no landslides, mudslides, faults, etc., which is, thus, suitable for construction of rural infrastructures such as road. The mid-subtropical monsoon humid climate is defined in Qijiang County, with abundant precipitation and distinct seasons and obvious three-dimensional climate. The annual average precipitation is 1200mm and temperature is 18 in the region. The prevailing wind is calm wind and northwest wind at an average velocity of 1m/s for many years. Qijiang River and Puhe River, originated from the Dalou Mountains in Guizhou, are two major rivers in the County, coming from the south and southeast and joining the Yangtze River in the north, and traveling at average flow rate of 1425m 3 /s. The underground water in Qijiang County is classified as fragmental rock pore crevice water, bedrock crevice water, loose rock pore water. The occurrence of fragmental rock pore crevice water and bedrock crevice water is identified in the Trias Feixianguan formation limestone and dolomite, exhibiting small amount and deep burial of underground water and weak connection on hydro power. The occurrence of loose rock 

pore water is identified in the Quaternary formation and bedrock’s highly weathered zone, Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 27 

District/ County Name of Sub-component Related Landform, Geomorphic, Meteorology, River and Hydrology 

with small amount of water and feeding from atmospheric precipitation and surface runoff. Wanzhou District New countryside demonstration in Wutu village in Dazhou town Wutu Village, 1.2km from the premise of Dazhou Town government, situated on the Yangtze River Grade I platform. Typically, crevice water or infiltration water is available in the region and flow in the way of spring water surrounding the Yangtze River and finally joins the Yangtze River. Chengkou County New countryside demonstration in Qinglong Village in Xianyi Typical mountain landforms are seen in Qinglong Village, located in the south of Chengkou County. Farmers live in mountains at sea level of 730-1300m, where the subtropical monsoon humid climate is defined, with abundant precipitation and distinct seasons. Hanchang River, traveling at an average flow rate of 20m 3 /s, flows from the east to the west with Qinglong Village at north and south sides. Zhongliubaodu River and Yangxi River are two tributaries of Hanchang River, which are spring stream arising from the underground water and flow at velocity of 3m 3 /s and 2.5m 3 /s. The underground water in the region is easily accessible at short distance. According to the hydrological survey on related regions and analogue assessment on quality of underground water in the neighboring construction sites, the affected underground water has not negative impact on concrete. 

Nan’an District Rural surplus labor employment transfer training project in Chongqing Technology & Business University The proposed base site is located at #208, Sigongli Street, Haitangxi, Nan’an District, which is an area previously controlled by Chongqing Yangtze Rubber Plant and adjoins the Xuefu avenue. The proposed site, bordering Chongqing Technology and Business University, is favorable for full utilization of the PIU’s education resources and management on the training base. Shizhu County Employment transfer training base for rural surplus labor Shizhu Vocational Education Center is located at #103, Wanshou Ave. Shizhu County. In view of domestic wastewater and domestic garbage possibly occurred in the construction at an early stage, a lot of measures on wastewater and solid waste treatment have been taken and various drainage network, greening and other environmental protection facilities have been built. Wushan County Employment transfer training base for rural surplus labor Wushan Vocational Education Center is located at Commune 6, Xiping Village, Goujiaping, Wuxia Town, Wushan County (Goujiaping Vocational Education Park in the north of Wushan new city). Wushan, situated in the core of Three Gorges reservoir surrounded by Daba Mountain in the northeast of Chongqing, is the east entrance to Chongqing, which is the 1 st county to be submerged within the Three Gorges Chongqing reservoir, a big immigrant relocation county and one of 14 key anti-poverty counties across the country. Wushan Vocational Education Center is designed to provide a platform for education of rural workforce in favor of re-employment. Employment transfer training base for rural surplus labor Yubei Vocational Education Center is located at Baotong 1 st branch, Huixing Street, Yubei District, Chongqing (land of former high-tech industrial park #51). Yubei District Community health care system The proposed site is located at F46-3/01 land block of Chongqing Yubei Airport New City and Yuelai New Town. Zhuchang stream, a small tributary of Jialing River, flows at 3km from the site in the west and Dashiba Reservoir is situated in the south. 

## 2.3 Availability of Water Resources 

Analysis of availability of water resources focuses on compliance of water quality at water source intake for each water supply sub-component with the water quality standard. As per the Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 28 

Water Quality Standard for Drinking Water Sources (CJ 3020-93), water quality for drinking water source is defined in two classes. Class I water source: Water quality is good. The underground water may be used for drinking only after disinfection process while the surface water may be used for drinking only after simple purification treatment (such as filtration) and disinfection. Class II water source: Water is slightly contaminated. Water quality can meet the requirements of Water Quality Standard for Drinking Water (GB5749-2006) after common purification treatment (flocculation, precipitation, infiltration, disinfection, etc.) and can be used for drinking. Water source beyond Class II limits shall not be used for drinking. If such water has to be used due to limited conditions, appropriate purification treatment measures shall be taken to make sure the compliance of quality of processed water with the stipulations of GB5749-2006 and the approval by the health department (bureau) at the provincial, municipal or autonomous region level and other related authorities shoule be obtained. Wangjiaping Water Treatment Plant in Fengjie County was put into operation in June 2003. Its water source is Huangjing Reservoir, the quality of which reaches Class II Standard. The water source can be used for drinking. Therefore, such water, when treated as per Water Quality Standard for Drinking Water (GB5749-2006) in Wangjiaping Water Plant, is acceptable for drinking water Fengjie Zhuyi area of water supply sub-component under CURIP. According to the monitoring data of Huangshui Lake in Shizhu County, all indicators except fecal coliforms and total phosphorus are within the limits of Class II water quality standard of 

Environmental Quality Standard for Surface Water (GB3838-2002), meeting the waters function requirements (total phosphorous meeting GB3838-2002 Class III water quality standard), and water quality function requirements on surface water drinking source water of Class II protection zone. Therefore, such water may be used as drinking source. Zhongling Reservoir in the vicinity of Shiye Town, Xiushan County is a medium-sized reservoir, where there is abundant precipitation. The dam sits at junction where the Zhongxi River joins the Meijiang River. The reservoir, built at the end of 1985, extends in Zhongling and Zhongxi villages, with catchment area of 92.6 km 2, dam crest elevation of 373.4m, max. Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 29 dam height of 54.46m, dam top of 250m in length and 7m in width, dam bottom of 270m in width. Its total capacity amounts to 32.3 million m 3 and average runoff for many years arrives at 68.1 million m 3. A power station is built at the back of the reservoir. The reservoir is owned by Xiushan Water Conservancy Bureau and managed by Zhongling reservoir office. In March, 2009, the PIU authorized Xiushan Disease Control and Prevention Center to check the water quality of diversion canal in Xujiaao water plant. Please see XSJKJZ (2009) No. 92 and No. 93 inspection reports for details. The examination results indicate that water in Zhongling Reservoir is good and such water may be used as water source for drinking in city and town and industrial application in industrial enterprises. There are abundant underground water reserves around Rongxi Town, Xiushan County, the types of which include carbonate karst water, and fragmental rock pore crevice water and Quaternary loose pore water. Development of limestone and dolomite karst in the region contributes to the most common karst water-bearing layer, characterized by development of various karst types, rich occurrence of underground water in karst crevice and pipe, thus forming uneven water-bearing layer, requiring large areas to be fed. A large amount of water is drained to low-lying grounds along the river. The fragmental rock and epirock are crevice water-bearing layers so as to permit occurrence of underground water in crevices and faults fracture zones. The spring water flows at a rate of 0.1-0.3litre/s, which is fed from atmospheric precipitation and drained to the valleys and low-lying grounds. The underground spring in Fengshen Cave, Dayuquan, Toudao River, has flow rate of 3850m 3 /d during the low water period. In March, 2009, the PIU authorized Xiushan Disease Control and Prevention Center to check the water quality of spring water source in Toudao River, Rongxi Town. Please see XSJKJZ [2009] No.104 and No. 105 inspection reports for details. The inspection results indicate that all indicators, except colon bacillus, thermotolerant coliform bacteria, are within the limits of Class II water quality standard of the Water Quality Standard for Drinking Water (GB5749-2006). The Shuaijia Reservoir in Longchi Town, Xiushan County is a key small water conservancy project designed to provide agricultural irrigation, as well as flood control and water supply, which is constructed with rain collection area of 4.4km 2, water drenching area of 12.0km 2, total capacity of 6.8 million m 3 and intended irrigation area of 22,000mu. The dam is a grouted rubble gravity-wall type rockfill dam with dam crest elevation of 560.0m, max. dam height of 18.2m, dam top of 89.76m in length and 4.0m in width. The reservoir is owned by Xiushan Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 30 Water Conservancy Bureau and managed by Shuaijia reservoir office. In March, 2009, the PIU authorized Xiushan Disease Control and Prevention Center to check the source water quality of Shuaijia reservoir. Please see XSJKJZ [2009] No. 102 and No. 103 inspection reports for details. The inspection results indicate that all indicators, except colon bacillus, thermotoletant coliform bacteria, are within the limits of Class II water quality standard of the Water Quality Standard for Drinking Water (GB5749-2006). Qijiang Daqiaokou Reservoir, 3000m from Qingshui Village, has abundant precipitation and satisfied water quality, without point source pollution and significant area source pollution. Inspection of raw water quality shows that all indicators, except colon bacillus, are within the limits of Class 2 water quality standard. Daqiaokou Reservoir, 1500m from Xishan Village and 23m higher than the Village, can realize water intake, water preparation and water transferring by gravity feed. The inspection of raw water quality shows that all indicators, except visual substances and colon bacillus, are within the limits of Class 2 water quality standard. The 

Meijiagou Reservoir is situated in the close vicinity of Chuijiao Village’s Dashan Village villager team and Huangshakan Village villager team. The inspection of raw water quality shows that all indicators, except visual substances and colon bacillus, are within the limits of Class II water quality standard. In Xifangtai villager team, the inspection of quality of underground spring water shows that all indicators, except colon bacillus, are good enough for drinking purpose. Qijiang River water is good enough to meet Class III water quality standard of Environmental Quality Standard for Surface Water (GB3838-2002). According to the reports of Chengkou Disease Control and Prevention Center on April 2 nd and 7th , 2009, the two spring streams of Zhongliubaodu River and Yangxi River in Qinglong Village, Xianyi Town, Chengkou County, are good enough to meet Class I requirements of 

Water Quality Standard for Drinking Water Sources (CJ 3020-93). Comparisons with the standards indicate that surface water or spring water in water sources related to the water supply projects are in good condition so that water resources for related water supply sub-components may be well utilized. Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 31 

## 2.4 Current Situation of Related Lake (Dam) of Water Supply Sub-component 

Water supply sub-components involve Huangshui Lake Grade-3 Lake in Shizhu County, Zhongling Reservoir in Xiushan County, Shuaijia Reservoir, Daqiaokou Reservoir in Qijiang County and Meijiagou Reservoir. The latter four with dam height of more than 15mmay involve dam safety issue. The water in the above reservoirs has been inspected by the authority concerned and can be used as drinking water sources. It is estimated that reconstruction of Shuaijia Reservoir and Meijiagou Reservoir will be finished by Sept. – Oct. 2009. The reservoirs are managed by the water conservancy bureau in the region respectively, and the flood control emergency plan have been mapped out for the dams. Fengjie County Zhuyi water supply (to be fed from Wangjiaping Water Plant) Huangjing Reservoir 

Huangjing Reservoir, located in Huangjing Village, Zhuyi Town, sits at -shaped turn of Zhierba in Zhuyi River, 13km from Zhuyi Town, 20.1km from the Yangtze River junction, with total capacity of 3.63million m 3 .Runoff across the dam amounts to 17.16 million m 3 at an average for years. The dam is a reinforced concrete panel rolling rockfill dam, with max. dam height of 47.3m, axial length of 184.23m on dam top and dam top width of 5m. The water in the reservoir is checked to meet Class II water quality standard and transferred to Wangjiaping Water Plant via 23km-long DN600 pipe in gravity feed. Approval of Fengjie Water Conservancy Bureau concerning Storage Safety Assessment of Huangjing Reservoir (FJS [2009] No. 74) concludes: Huangjing Reservoir, small (type I) reservoir, meets the requirements in Technical Code for Safety Monitoring of Earth and Rockfill Dam in terms of safety monitoring design and measurement arrangement. The dam’s upstream face is a vertical face with back slope ratio of 1:0.7. Seismic intensity VI degree is defined in the dam area. The dam is managed by Shizhu Water Conservancy Bureau. 3 persons are designated for the safety of the dam. Xiushan County Water supply to Shiye Town Zhongling Reservoir Situated in Kaibao Village, Zhongling Town, and 33km from the county downtown, Zhongling Reservoir is designed with rain collection area of 92.6km 2 , capacity of 32.3million m 3 , average runoff of 68.1million m 3 for years and intended irrigation area of 60,000mu. Water supply to Longchi Town Shuaijia Reservoir Situated in Tongmu Village, Longchi Town, and 26km from the county downtown, Shuaijia Reservoir, which construction started in 1971 and finished in 1985, is designed with rain collection area of 16.4km 2 , capacity of 6.8million m 3 , average runoff of 8.51million m 3 for years and intended irrigation area of 22,000mu. Since rain drenching works is not completed without complete canal system, no irrigation is put in place so that the reservoir is mainly used for drinking. The dam is a grouted rubble gravity dam, with dam height of 18.2m. Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 36 Table 2-4 indicates that there are two categories of surface water environment associated with road project: The first category refers to regions where surface water quality is highly beyond the limits, thus requiring much improvement of integrated environment in neighboring areas. For example, the quality of water across the section of Wanshouqiao, Yuxi River, Banan District, is pretty undesirable, with COD, BOD 5, TP, petroleum and fecal coliforms largely beyond the limits. Thus, such water is not good enough to meet Class III water quality standard. Bachuan River in Tongliang County fails to meet the requirements due to COD, BOD 5 and petroleum beyond the limits. The water in the Xiaoanxi River meets the requirements of Class III water quality standard of Environmental Quality Standard for Surface Water (GB3838-2002) except for coliform group in the section through Tongliang County and BOD 5 in the section through Yongchuan District. The other category refers to good quality of surface water in rivers or reservoirs in remote regions. For example, the water in the Zhuyi River in Fengjie County meets the limits of Class III water quality standard of Environmental Quality Standard for Surface Water (GB3838-2002) except for fecal coliforms. The water in Longhe River in Shizhu County, Meijiang River in Xiushan County and Rongxi River are good enough to meet the requirements of Class II and III water quality standards of Environmental Quality Standard for Surface Water (GB3838-2002). Water quality of the Yangtze River meets Class III water quality standard except for fecal coliforms. The project of health care system in Yubei District may get access to surface water source in the neighboring Zhuchang River and Dashiba Reservoir. Except for total phosphorous beyond Class IV standard due to agricultural area source pollution, the Dashiba Reservoir water meets the standard. In water source locations for water supply sub-components, the surface water has desirable environment quality and thus can be used as drinking water (see Section 2.4.3). ## 2.6 Socio-Economic Environment 2.6.1 General 

Chongqing became the municipality directly under the central government in 1997. By seizing the historic opportunities of the Three Gorges Project and the Western Development, Chongqing set the strategy of “one circle and two wings” and focused on the one-hour economic circle in order to greatly adjust economic structure, open wide to the outside world, Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 45 intensify institutional reform and accelerate infrastructure construction. It witnesses overall economic and social development and further increase the comprehensive strength. Chongqing is the important economic growth pole in West China and one of the six big industrial bases in China. Its comprehensive strength is in the lead among provinces and cities in West China. Chongqing municipality ranks fifth among the 12 at provincial level in terms of economic aggregate in West China, while its city proper ranks first. Chongqing is also a combination of large urban and rural areas. The rural area covers a wide area and has a large population. After ten-year development, noticeable changes took place in Chongqing’s economy and society. Conflicts in the dualistic urban-rural structure have been improved on certain degree. Great achievement has been obtained in the new countryside construction. A series of policies and measures that facilitate the new countryside construction have been put into operation. During the “10 th Five-year Plan” period, 2.6 million rural surplus workers have been transferred, the planning preparation for 1,000 villages has been finished and the construction of 3,000 public service centers has been launched. In 2008 Chongqing responded actively to the adverse impact of the international financial crisis, strived to promote the urban-rural integration reform, construction of inland open highland and development of “Five Chongqings” and did its best for the earthquake relief work to realize stable and healthy economic and social development of the municipality. According to the initial estimation, Chongqing accomplished gross regional product (GRP) of RMB 509.666 billion in 2008 with 14.3% increase compared with last year, in which the added value of the primary industry scored RMB 57.54 billion with an increase of 6.8%, which made up 11.3% of the GRP, 0.4 percentage-point drop over last year; the secondary industry scored RMB 243.327 billion with an increase of 18.0%, which made up 47.7% of the GRP, 1.8 percentage-point rise over last year; and the tertiary industry scored RMB 208.799 billion with an increase of 12.4%, which made up 41.0% of the GRP, 1.4 percentage-point drop over last year. The GRP per capita (according to resident population) reached RMB 18,025, rising by 13.7% compared with last year. From the regional aspect, the GRP of the one-hour economic circle reached RMB 395,927 billion with an increase of 13.6% over last year and making up 77.7% of the total, 0.36 percentage-point rise over last year, that of the “two-wing” area – Northeast Chongqing and Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 46 Southeast Chongqing reached RMB 85.699 billion and RMB 28.04 billion with an increase of 17.1% and 16.4% over last year and making up 16.8% and 5.5% of the total. The proportion of 

the “two-wing” area rises by 0.36 percentage point. The total traffic mileage of road in 2008 was up to 108,600km, including expressway of 1,165km. 85.7% of the roads of administrative villages were accessible, increasing by 13.9 percentage points over last year, while 38.9% were clear, increasing by 11 percentage points. There were 396km urban road constructed and reconstructed in 2008, 256km water supply pipeline constructed, comprehensive water supply capacity of 42,000t/d increased, 222,000m³/d sewage treatment capacity increased, 1,732 community service facilities and 152 comprehensive community service centers constructed. There were 2,258 health care agencies built up by the end of 2008, including 1,396 hospitals and health care institutions, 40 maternal and child care service centers/stations, 13 specialized disease prevention and treatment center/stations, 43 centers for disease control and prevention and 42 health monitoring institutions. The standard health care institutions in towns account for 51.9% of the total. Hospitals and health care institutions were equipped with 77,900 beds and 88,700 health care workers, including 39,400 medical practitioners and assistant medical practitioners and 26,800 registered nurses. 21.75 million peasants were covered in the new rural cooperative medical care system. 

## 2.6.2 Necessity of Implementing CURIP 

Though great achievement was obtained in the socioeconomic development, Chongqing still faces enormous challenges. Since Chongqing is a combination of big urban area, rural area, reservoir area, mountain area and ethnic minority area, there are still 21 million population living in rural area, problems such as lack of clean drinking water, safe road for production and living, and sound health care and educational services still exist. There is acute imbalance in the dualistic urban-rural structure and there is a long way to go for urban-rural integration development. The urbanization increased the inequity of urban and rural incomes, while improving the urban-rural infrastructure as well as people’s livelihood. The statistic data showed that the disposable income per capita of urban residents scored RMB 10,244 in 2005, which increased by 12.9% to RMB 11,570 in 2006, and scored RMB 15,218 in 2008 with an increase of 13.2% over 2007, while the net income of the peasants per capita over the same period reached RMB 2,809, RMB Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 47 2,874 and RMB 4,126, increasing by 2.3% and 17.6% respectively. The net income of the peasants per capita in 2006 and 2008 accounted for only 24.8% and 27.1% of that of the urban residents. The public service system is not included in the former statistics on income of urban people, so the actual gap between rural and urban area is even bigger. Imbalanced rural-urban public service is mainly due to its different rights, different qualities, different quantities and different prices. The urban-rural integration development and reform becomes more and more urgent. It is of necessity to strengthen construction of infrastructure and public service facilities in small towns and villages, including road, water supply, sewage treatment, waste disposal, basic health care and medical service system and employment capacity upgrade for migrant workers, to make a change to the status that the public service is centralized in big cities and make a breakthrough to the city-led public service structure, in order to sum up experience in urban-rural integration development and provide demonstration to narrow down the gap between urban and rural areas. It is of great necessity to implement the relevant sub-components as the first-batch pilot projects under CURIP, based on the following analysis. 

2.6.2.1 Road and Water Supply Infrastructure in Town to Be Improved 

Though the road accessibility and unobstruction rate in administrative villages of Chongqing has been improved, the road backwardness has yet to be changed fundamentally. Low supply, low industrial grade, importance given to construction more than maintenance and low accessibility for passengers are the main problems exist in rural road development. Banan District which is part of the city proper has both the characteristics of rural and urban areas. Of the current 2,026km road, there is only 132km above Class 2 level and the clay-bound macadam makes up 87% of the total mileage. Most of the rural roads can’t meet the standard and there are 35 administrative villages inaccessible by road. The roads connecting Huimin County, Nanpeng County and Yipin County are of Class 3, Class 4 or can’t meet standard, with low technical class. Due to the comparatively developed economy, high population density and accelerated urbanization, its traffic flow begins to increase and problems concerning safety of transportation between towns and resident trips are marked. The quality and quantity of the existing roads can not meet the requirements for socioeconomic development now and in the future. Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 48 National Highway 319 and 326 run through the center area of Xiushan County. Due to large traffic flow (average daily traffic flow in a year: 18,810/d) and low industrial standard, the road pavement has been greatly damaged. The congestion caused by pedestrian and vehicles has influenced the traffic capacity and safety of the residents. Complaints can be heard everywhere. 

Mie’ao to Liangting Road in Xiushan County is the through-traffic section of National Highway 319 and 326 and also the link of road and railway transportation in Xiushan County. This road project can facilitate the through-traffic road and railway transportation, ease the traffic pressure of the county and promote the development of industrial park at city level of Xiushan’s characteristics. The existing low-cost roads in Banqiao County, Yongchuan District are almost at village level, mainly used by motorcycles and agricultural vehicles. Some sections fail to run through due to various reasons and become dead-end roads. Some sections have large slope with small curve radius of plane, influencing the traffic capacity and safety of the residents, which can not meet the requirements of ever-growing economy, flow of people and logistics and restrict the regional economic development and the growth of production and living standard of the public. The inadequate infrastructure, especially inadequate water supply and poor water quality, has greatly impacted the living standard and health of the people and restricted development of towns and improvement of investment environment. According to data provided by the health agencies, the water supply of 80% towns can not reach the sanitation standard and 12.73 million people are waiting for safe drinking water, 4.95 million of whom are facing water scarcity. The water supply station in Shiye Town, Xiushan County, built up in 1980s, uses the surface water as the water source, which is not effectively treated before entering the pipeline network and can not meet the requirement of Water Quality Standard for Drinking Water Sources . Its water supply capacity is 200m 3/d only. Its service hours are totaled to 3 hours every day covering about 10,000 people. The output can not satisfy the demand of the residents. Every household has constructed water tank for water storage, in which case, the secondary pollution is inevitable and the basic living conditions and even the production conditions can not be guaranteed. The Zhuyi Area of Wanzhou District is the main area for settlement of migrants from Fengjie County due to the Three Gorges Reservoir Construction. The water supply project is planned to Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 49 improve the living and production conditions of the settled migrants from Three Gorges area. However, the water supply pipelines for the Zhuyi Area have not been laid yet, which would become the major restriction for the regional social stability and continuous economic development. It is urgent to launch the water supply project in Zhuyi Area. The water supply facilities in Huangshui Town, Shizhu County also can not satisfy the demand of the residents for living and trade due to intensified urbanization, expanded tourism and construction of other bases for agricultural production. Water supply has become the bottleneck for urban-rural economic development of Huangshui Town. Xianyi in Chengkou County is located in a remote mountain area. Its infrastructure is generally not well developed and the basic conditions in the rural area are not favorable. The drinking water project in Qinglong Village for new countryside construction can facilitate the improvement of basic living conditions of rural residents. Qijiang County plans to expand and harden the original trunk roads and tractor roads in Qingshui Village, Xishan Village and Chuijiao Village and to introduce water from the mountain area in line with the local conditions, so as to solve the basic problems related to people’s livelihood, such as traffic difficulties, transportation difficulties and water scarcity for both people and livestock. 

2.6.2.2 Job Opportunities for Migrant Workers to be Increased 

According to the 2008 statistics issued by Chongqing Investigation Brigade of National Bureau of Statistics of China, the expedited construction of new countryside for urban-rural integration development and reform increases the job opportunities in Chongqing and more farmers are willing to work in Chongqing. Therefor, the workers can give consideration to both farm and off farm work. The favorable momentum of economic growth in Chongqing also greatly 

attracts migrant workers. The survey “Are you willing to work in other cities after the Spring Festival” shows that 63.8% of the surplus rural labor prefers to work in Chongqing. It is more and more difficult for migrant workers to find a foothold in urban areas just by their hardwork and diligence. According to the statistics on posts for migrant workers in Nanjimen Migrant Worker Service Market in Yuzhong District, Chongqing, more than 85% of the posts are for junior middle school graduates, 25% of which are for senior middle school graduates; more than 56% of the posts are for qualified primary workers and 60% of them are for skilled Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 50 workers. Such requirements on education and skills are just for migrant workers in Chongqing, while the requirements are higher in coastal cities. However, according to the statistics by Chongqing Municipal Administration on Employment, by the end of 2008, 6.78 million of the 7.78 million migrant workers in Chongqing had not graduated from junior middle schools, accounting for 87% of the total; only 1.74 million of those migrant workers had received trainings, accounting for 22% of the total, and a few of them had gotten the skill certificate. Lack of relevant skills makes it somewhat difficult for migrant workers to find a job. Many migrant workers are rushing to the city proper of Chongqing from Chongqing 

Municipality and adjacent regions, and Chongqing’s migrant workers that used to work in coastal cities before 2008 also go back to adjacent cities for work. It is expected that the migrant workers will increase by 1 million persons/year in the future several years, and Chongqing Municipal People’s Government takes it the most important task to facilitate migrant workers’ life and work. The employment transfer training bases for rural surplus labor will help migrant workers, especially the poor-educated ones and middle-aged ones with poor study ability, get more job opportunities through practical skill trainings and be finally employed in the city proper through equal competition with urban residents. In addition, this may solve some problems: in certain areas, the technical trainings achieved remarkable social effect, but the financial return is low, therefore it’s difficult to continue such training courses. Under such kind of situation, skilled worker shortage and unskilled worker surplus coexist. 

2.6.2.3 Basic Health Care Systems in Chongqing to be Reformed and to Improve their Performance 

The population in rural areas of Chongqing has reached 21 million. Health care institutions have been established in about 78% of the villages and towns and have covered 90% of the poor counties. However, the health care service in communities, especially in rural areas, is still in the primary stage. Less sophisticated facilities and unskilled employees make poor performance. Thus the public do not rely on it and prefer to go to a large hospital even if they are just slightly sick. As a result, there are too many patients in large hospitals while small hospitals are empty. That causes a huge waste of medical resources. According to the true and reliable data from village-level and town-level health care stations and community health care centers collected and accepted by the WB mssion, the distribution and performance of medical and health care service in Chongqing need to be improved in accordance with Table 2-11 and Table 2-12. Fee-for-Service is adopted for basic health insurance and urban health insurance in Chongqing. The practice proves that the Fee-for-Service will stimulate the service provider to lead or even 

request, making use of the patient’s disadvantage that he/she is in the health-care blind spot, the patient to increase the demands for medical service. However, the service charges increased are paid by the patient, and the high charges may make the low-income people poorer. Therefore, the reform in health care fields focuses on such rural areas as villages and towns. One of the applicable measures is the investment to reduce the gap between urban and rural medical and health care service, i.e. the difference of infrastructures and medical equipments. Capacity building of basic health care systems to be initiated in Chongqing includes procurement of community medical and health care equipments, building of village-level health care offices in villages (jointly built with villages’ public service centers), trainings of general practitioners and soft science research. Therefore, it is necessary to include the capacity building of basic health care systems into the CURIP. Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 52

The ground is leveled in accordance with sections, and the fill is used for the excavated place. Along the roads, there are 12 dumping places which are all at the depressed wasteland. The distance between the fill and the dumping area is not more than 0.5km. The earth and stone shall be kept balanced. The dumping area is built with block stones. The village committee relevant to the road section shall be responsible for using the discarded earth and stone to level the low lying land, reclaim the cropland or plant trees. ## 3.3.2 Analysis of Main Pollutants of CURIP 

Please see Table 3-4 for main pollutants of CURIP. In Table 3-4, the flying dust during construction is mainly caused by the carrying vehicles and bulk material storage yards. On condition that the air is fully dry, the flying dust resulting from the carrying vehicles can be calculated according to the following empirical formula: 75 .085 .0 )5.0/()8.6/)( 5/(123 .0 PWVQ =

WHERE: Q - dust caused by the carrying vehicle, kg/km·vehicle V - speed of vehicle, km/hr W - loading capacity of vehicle, t P - amount of dust on the road surface, kg/m 2

Therefore, the higher the vehicle speed is, the more the dust is on the road and the more the flying dust will be. It is necessary to place a speed limit for the vehicles, keep the road surface clean or moistened, so as to reduce the flying dust. According to the experience from site construction, the flying dust caused by the carrying vehicles accounts for over 60% of total flying dust. In the event of dry and windy climate, the bulk material storage yards on the construction site will cause the flying dust which can be calculated according to the following empirical formula:

When the particle diameter of bulk 

materials is 250 m, the falling velocity is 1.005m/s. When the diameter of tiny dust particles is less than 250 m, it is difficult for them to fall to the ground, thus exerting great influence on the 

environment. So the best way to reduce the flying dust is to reduce the bulk materials piled in the open air and ensure certain water content. Summary of Environmental Impact Assessment Report for CURIP 68 Table 3-4 Analysis of Main Pollutants in CURIP
